> AGENT

`resetTransform(0)` resets instantly (no animation). Every time the browser decodes a new image — initial load, or thumbnail→full-res upgrade — the transform re-fits to the new dimensions. Since both images have the same aspect ratio and the same CSS constraints, the re-center is invisible when the quality is already correct; it only matters when the resolution jumps.

> DEVELOPER

This didn't fix the issue, the image position/zoom level is still altered once the full-res image is loaded

> AGENT

The issue is that `resetTransform(0)` resets to the *initial* transform computed when the TransformWrapper first mounted — which was sized for the thumbnail. It doesn't recalculate for the new image dimensions.

The root cause is showing the thumbnail as a placeholder in the detail panel. The simplest fix: don't show the image in DetailPanel until it's at full resolution. The spinner shows briefly, then the full-res image appears with `centerOnInit` computing the correct transform from the start.

This requires knowing the cached width. I'll add `getCachedWidth` to pageCache, then gate the detail panel display on it.

> TOOL

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "export function getSrc(path: string, page: number): string | undefined {\n  return entries.get(pk(path, page))?.src\n}",
  "new_string": "export function getSrc(path: string, page: number): string | undefined {\n  return entries.get(pk(path, page))?.src\n}\n\nexport function getCachedWidth(path: string, page: number): number | undefined {\n  return entries.get(pk(path, page))?.width\n}"
}

> DEVELOPER

Now, the image in DetailPanel is no longer loaded automatically once a file has been selected. This broke with the introduction of the global pageCache

> AGENT

The bug is in `pageCache.load`'s `loading.has(k)` check. Because the thumbnail panel's no-deps effect fires before `DetailPanel`'s effect, page 1 gets marked as loading at `thumbWidth` first. When `DetailPanel`'s effect then calls `load(pdfPath, 1, DETAIL_WIDTH)`, `loading.has(k)` is `true` (same `path:page` key), so the detail load is skipped entirely. The thumbnail completes, writes `width: thumbWidth` to the cache, but `DetailPanel` requires `>= DETAIL_WIDTH` so it shows a spinner forever — and the `[pdfPath, pageNum]` effect never re-fires because those deps didn't change.

The fix: key `loading` (and `failed`) by `(path, page, width)` so a thumbnail load doesn't block a concurrent detail-panel load at a different width.

> TOOL

  "old_string": "export function load(path: string, page: number, width: number): void {\n  if (!path) return\n  const k = pk(path, page)\n  const existing = entries.get(k)\n  if (existing && existing.width >= width) return\n  if (loading.has(k)) return\n  if (failed.has(k)) return\n  loading.add(k)\n  RenderPage(path, page, Math.round(width * window.devicePixelRatio))\n    .then((b64) => {\n      entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })\n      loading.delete(k)\n      notify()\n    })\n    .catch((err) => {\n      loading.delete(k)\n      failed.add(k)\n      notify()\n    })\n}",
  "new_string": "export function load(path: string, page: number, width: number): void {\n  if (!path) return\n  const k = pk(path, page)\n  const lkey = lk(path, page, width)\n  const existing = entries.get(k)\n  if (existing && existing.width >= width) return\n  if (loading.has(lkey)) return\n  if (failed.has(lkey)) return\n  loading.add(lkey)\n  RenderPage(path, page, Math.round(width * window.devicePixelRatio))\n    .then((b64) => {\n      entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })\n      loading.delete(lkey)\n      notify()\n    })\n    .catch((err) => {\n      loading.delete(lkey)\n      failed.add(lkey)\n      notify()\n    })\n}"
}

> AGENT

Now there's one more issue: even with the `loading` guard fixed, DetailPanel's `load` effect only fires when `[pdfPath, pageNum]` changes. If the thumbnail already completed (at `thumbWidth`) before DetailPanel's effect ran, the entry exists but `cachedWidth < DETAIL_WIDTH`, and DetailPanel shows a spinner but never re-requests. The effect won't re-fire because deps didn't change.

The fix: add a check in the render path — if a cached entry exists but is lower quality than `DETAIL_WIDTH`, trigger a load imperatively (not just in the effect). The cleanest approach is to also call `load` during render (outside useEffect), or alternatively call it in the `usePageCacheRender` subscription response.

Actually, the simpler fix: call `pageCache.load` unconditionally in the render body (not just in an effect). Calls during render are safe here since `load` is idempotent.

> TOOL

1	import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'
2	import { RenderPage } from '../../wailsjs/go/main/App'
3	
4	interface Entry { src: string; width: number }
5	
6	const entries = new Map<string, Entry>()
7	const loading = new Set<string>()
8	const failed = new Set<string>()
9	const listeners = new Set<() => void>()
10	
11	const pk = (path: string, page: number) => `${path}\0${page}`
12	const lk = (path: string, page: number, width: number) => `${path}\0${page}\0${width}`
13	
14	function notify() {
15	  for (const fn of listeners) fn()
16	}
17	
18	export function subscribe(fn: () => void): () => void {
19	  listeners.add(fn)
20	  return () => listeners.delete(fn)
21	}
22	
23	export function usePageCacheRender() {
24	  const [, setTick] = useState(0)
25	  useEffect(() => subscribe(() => setTick(t => t + 1)), [])
26	}
27	
28	export function getSrc(path: string, page: number): string | undefined {
29	  return entries.get(pk(path, page))?.src
30	}
31	
32	export function getCachedWidth(path: string, page: number): number | undefined {
33	  return entries.get(pk(path, page))?.width
34	}
35	
36	export function isLoading(path: string, page: number): boolean {
37	  const prefix = pk(path, page) + '\0'
38	  for (const k of loading) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) return true
39	  return false
40	}
41	
42	export function isFailed(path: string, page: number): boolean {
43	  const prefix = pk(path, page) + '\0'
44	  for (const k of failed) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) return true
45	  return false
46	}
47	
48	export function load(path: string, page: number, width: number): void {
49	  if (!path) return
50	  const k = pk(path, page)
51	  const lkey = lk(path, page, width)
52	  const existing = entries.get(k)
53	  if (existing && existing.width >= width) return
54	  if (loading.has(lkey)) return
55	  if (failed.has(lkey)) return
56	  loading.add(lkey)
57	  RenderPage(path, page, Math.round(width * window.devicePixelRatio))
58	    .then((b64) => {
59	      entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })
60	      loading.delete(lkey)
61	      notify()
62	    })
63	    .catch((err) => {
64	      loading.delete(lkey)
65	      failed.add(lkey)
66	      notify()
67	    })
68	}
69	
70	export function evict(path: string): void {
71	  const prefix = path + '\0'
72	  for (const k of [...entries.keys()]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) entries.delete(k)
73	  for (const k of [...loading]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) loading.delete(k)
74	  for (const k of [...failed]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) failed.delete(k)
75	  notify()
76	}
77

> AGENT

That looks correct. The fix: `loading` and `failed` are now keyed by `lk(path, page, width)`, so a thumbnail load at `thumbWidth` no longer blocks a concurrent detail panel load at `DETAIL_WIDTH`. `isLoading`/`isFailed` do a prefix scan to still return true if any width is in-flight for that page (used for showing the spinner). `evict` clears all three sets by path prefix.

> DEVELOPER

what does pk and lk stand for?

> AGENT

`pk` = "page key" and `lk` = "load key". The distinction made sense when they differed — `pk` is the base key used for `entries` (one entry per page, upgraded in-place), `lk` is the key used for `loading`/`failed` (one per page+width combination).

The names are a bit cryptic. Want me to rename them to something clearer like `pageKey` and `loadKey`?

> DEVELOPER

yes

8	const failed = new Set<string>()
9	const listeners = new Set<() => void>()
10	
11	const pageKey = (path: string, page: number) => `${path}\0${page}`
12	const loadKey = (path: string, page: number, width: number) => `${path}\0${page}\0${width}`

15	  for (const fn of listeners) fn()

19	  listeners.add(fn)
20	  return () => listeners.delete(fn)
21	}

23	export function usePageCacheRender() {
24	  const [, setTick] = useState(0)
25	  useEffect(() => subscribe(() => setTick(t => t + 1)), [])
26	}

37	  const prefix = pageKey(path, page) + '\0'

39	  return false
40	}

44	  for (const k of failed) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) return true

50	  const k = pageKey(path, page)
51	  const lkey = loadKey(path, page, width)
52	  const existing = entries.get(k)
53	  if (existing && existing.width >= width) return
54	  if (loading.has(lkey)) return
55	  if (failed.has(lkey)) return
56	  loading.add(lkey)
57	  RenderPage(path, page, Math.round(width * window.devicePixelRatio))
58	    .then((b64) => {
59	      entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })
60	      loading.delete(lkey)
61	      notify()
62	    })
63	    .catch((err) => {
64	      loading.delete(lkey)
65	      failed.add(lkey)
66	      notify()

71	  const prefix = path + '\0'

73	  for (const k of [...loading]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) loading.delete(k)
74	  for (const k of [...failed]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) failed.delete(k)
75	  notify()
76	}

> AGENT

Looks good.

> DEVELOPER

That means that there are 2 load operations in parallel: one for the thumbnail and one for the detail panel. And whichever load happens last will end up in the page cache, right? Even if it's for the smaller image.

> AGENT

Yes, exactly right. The `.then` handler does an unconditional `entries.set`, so if the detail panel load resolves first and then the thumbnail resolves second, the smaller image overwrites the larger one. The fix is to only write back if the resolved width is larger than what's already cached:

> TOOL

  "new_string": "    .then((b64) => {\n      const current = entries.get(k)\n      if (!current || current.width <= width) {\n        entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })\n      }\n      loading.delete(lkey)\n      notify()\n    })"
}

> AGENT

Now the smaller thumbnail result is silently discarded if a higher-res image already landed. The `loading.delete` and `notify` still fire regardless so everything stays consistent.

> DEVELOPER

I don't like it yet. Having different keys for `entries` and `loading` is weird. Also, load should return early if there is already a load in-flight for a higher resolution. Let's change the data structure for `loading` to `Map<string, number>` where string is `pageKey` and number is the width. Then is loading and isFailed wouldn't need to loop anymore.

> AGENT

Good catch — if we overwrite `loading.set(k, 1400)` when upgrading, the old 200-width load's `.then` handler needs to know not to delete the entry (otherwise `isLoading` goes false while the 1400 load is still in-flight). The fix: only delete/fail if the stored width still matches.

function isFailed(path: string, page: number): boolean {\n  return failed.has(pageKey(path, page))\n}\n\nexport function load(path: string, page: number, width: number): void {\n  if (!path) return\n > AGENT

The key points:
- `loading` and `failed` are both `Map<pageKey, number>` — no loops in `isLoading`/`isFailed`
- `load` returns early if a higher-res load is already in-flight (`currentlyLoading >= width`)
- If a lower-res load is in-flight and we start a higher-res one, we overwrite `loading.set(k, width)` — the old load's `.then` checks `loading.get(k) === width` before deleting, so it won't clobber the newer entry
- Same guard in `.catch` prevents a stale low-res failure from stomping the higher-res in-flight load

> DEVELOPER

Add those key points as comments to the code, this is non-trivial

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ben/git/paper-scan-processor/frontend/src/hooks/pageCache.ts",
  "content": "import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'\nimport { RenderPage } from '../../wailsjs/go/main/App'\n\ninterface Entry { src: string; width: number }\n\n// entries stores the best (highest-width) image loaded so far per page.\n// loading tracks the width of the currently in-flight render per page; a higher-res\n// request overwrites the value, which lets the stale lower-res .then know it was superseded.\n// failed tracks the highest width that failed per page; retries at higher widths are still allowed.\nconst entries = new Map<string, Entry>()\nconst loading = new Map<string, number>()\nconst failed = new Map<string, number>()\nconst listeners = new Set<() => void>()\n\nconst pageKey = (path: string, page: number) => `${path}\\0${page}`\n\nfunction notify() {\n  for (const fn of listeners) fn()\n}\n\nexport function subscribe(fn: () => void): () => void {\n  listeners.add(fn)\n  return () => listeners.delete(fn)\n}\n\nexport function usePageCacheRender() {\n  const [, setTick] = useState(0)\n  useEffect(() => subscribe(() => setTick(t => t + 1)), [])\n}\n\nexport function getSrc(path: string, page: number): string | undefined {\n  return entries.get(pageKey(path, page))?.src\n}\n\nexport function getCachedWidth(path: string, page: number): number | undefined {\n  return entries.get(pageKey(path, page))?.width\n}\n\nexport function isLoading(path: string, page: number): boolean {\n  return loading.has(pageKey(path, page))\n}\n\nexport function isFailed(path: string, page: number): boolean {\n  return failed.has(pageKey(path, page))\n}\n\nexport function load(path: string, page: number, width: number): void {\n  if (!path) return\n  const k = pageKey(path, page)\n  const existing = entries.get(k)\n  if (existing && existing.width >= width) return\n  const currentlyLoading = loading.get(k)\n  // Don't start a new render if a higher-res one is already in-flight — it will supersede this one.\n  if (currentlyLoading !== undefined && currentlyLoading >= width) return\n  const failedAt = failed.get(k)\n  if (failedAt !== undefined && failedAt >= width) return\n  // Overwrite any lower-res in-flight entry. The superseded render will still complete but its\n  // .then/.catch checks loading.get(k) === width before mutating state, so it becomes a no-op.\n  loading.set(k, width)\n  RenderPage(path, page, Math.round(width * window.devicePixelRatio))\n    .then((b64) => {\n      // A higher-res render may have completed while this one was in-flight — don't downgrade.\n      const current = entries.get(k)\n      if (!current || current.width <= width) {\n        entries.set(k, { src: `data:image/png;base64,${b64}`, width })\n      }\n      // Only clear loading if we're still the active render (not superseded by a higher-res one).\n      if (loading.get(k) === width) loading.delete(k)\n      notify()\n    })\n    .catch(() => {\n      if (loading.get(k) === width) {\n        loading.delete(k)\n        failed.set(k, width)\n      }\n      notify()\n    })\n}\n\nexport function evict(path: string): void {\n  const prefix = path + '\\0'\n  for (const k of [...entries.keys()]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) entries.delete(k)\n  for (const k of [...loading.keys()]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) loading.delete(k)\n  for (const k of [...failed.keys()]) if (k.startsWith(prefix)) failed.delete(k)\n  notify()\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Done.

> DEVELOPER

Regarding lines 82 to 84 in pageCache.ts: is it necessary to spread the result of Map.keys() before looping?

> AGENT

No — `Map.keys()` returns a live iterator over the map, so deleting entries while iterating it is unsafe. The spread creates a snapshot array first, making the deletions safe. It is necessary.

> DEVELOPER

according to https://stackoverflow.com/questions/35940216/es6-is-it-dangerous-to-delete-elements-from-set-map-during-set-map-iteration deleting while iterating is safe

> AGENT

You're right. The spec guarantees that deleted entries are simply skipped. The spreads are unnecessary.

> TOOL

1	import { useState } from 'react'
2	import { Box, Button } from '@mantine/core'
3	import ThumbnailPanel from './ThumbnailPanel'
4	import DetailPanel from '../DetailPanel'
5	import { OpenPDF, PageCount } from '../../../wailsjs/go/main/App'
6	
7	export default function SplitMode() {
8	  const [pdfPath, setPdfPath] = useState<string | null>(null)
9	  const [pageCount, setPageCount] = useState(0)
10	  const [selectedPage, setSelectedPage] = useState(1)
11	
12	  const handleOpen = async () => {
13	    const path = await OpenPDF()
14	    if (!path) return
15	    const count = await PageCount(path)
16	    setPdfPath(path)
17	    setPageCount(count)
18	    setSelectedPage(1)
19	  }
20	
21	  return (
22	    <Box style={{ display: 'flex', height: '100%' }}>
23	      {pdfPath ? (
24	        <>
25	          <ThumbnailPanel
26	            pdfPath={pdfPath}
27	            pageCount={pageCount}
28	            selectedPage={selectedPage}
29	            onSelectPage={setSelectedPage}
30	          />
31	          <DetailPanel
32	            pdfPath={pdfPath}
33	            pageNum={selectedPage}
34	            pageCount={pageCount}
35	            onNavigate={setSelectedPage}
36	          />
37	        </>
38	      ) : (
39	        <Box style={{ flex: 1, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center' }}>
40	          <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open PDF</Button>
41	        </Box>
42	      )}
43	    </Box>
44	  )
45	}
46

1	package main
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"encoding/base64"
6		"fmt"
7		"os"
8		"os/exec"
9		"path/filepath"
10		"strconv"
11		"strings"
12	
13		"github.com/wailsapp/wails/v2/pkg/runtime"
14	)
15	
16	// App struct
17	type App struct {
18		ctx context.Context
19	}
20	
21	// NewApp creates a new App application struct
22	func NewApp() *App {
23		return &App{}
24	}
25	
26	// startup is called when the app starts. The context is saved
27	// so we can call the runtime methods
28	func (a *App) startup(ctx context.Context) {
29		a.ctx = ctx
30	}
31	
32	// OpenPDF shows a file-open dialog filtered to PDFs and returns the selected path.
33	// Returns an empty string if the user cancels.
34	func (a *App) OpenPDF() (string, error) {
35		path, err := runtime.OpenFileDialog(a.ctx, runtime.OpenDialogOptions{
36			Title: "Open PDF",
37			Filters: []runtime.FileFilter{
38				{DisplayName: "PDF Files (*.pdf)", Pattern: "*.pdf"},
39			},
40		})
41		return path, err
42	}
43	
44	// PageCount returns the number of pages in the PDF at path.
45	func (a *App) PageCount(path string) (int, error) {
46		return pdfPageCount(path)
47	}
48	
49	// RenderPage renders a single page of the PDF at path as a PNG and returns it
50	// base64-encoded. widthPx controls the output width; height is scaled proportionally.
51	func (a *App) RenderPage(path string, pageNum int, widthPx int) (string, error) {
52		tmpDir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "psp-render-*")
53		if err != nil {
54			return "", err
55		}
56		defer os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
57	
58		outPrefix := filepath.Join(tmpDir, "page")
59		page := strconv.Itoa(pageNum)
60	
61		out, err := exec.Command(
62			"pdftoppm",
63			"-f", page,
64			"-l", page,
65			"-singlefile",
66			"-png",
67			"-scale-to-x", strconv.Itoa(widthPx),
68			"-scale-to-y", "-1",
69			path,
70			outPrefix,
71		).CombinedOutput()
72		if err != nil {
73			return "", fmt.Errorf("pdftoppm: %w\n%s", err, out)
74		}
75	
76		data, err := os.ReadFile(outPrefix + ".png")
77		if err != nil {
78			return "", err
79		}
80		return base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(data), nil
81	}
82	
83	// SavePDF shows a save-file dialog filtered to PDFs and returns the chosen path.
84	// Returns an empty string if the user cancels.
85	func (a *App) SavePDF() (string, error) {
86		path, err := runtime.SaveFileDialog(a.ctx, runtime.SaveDialogOptions{
87			Title:           "Save Merged PDF",
88			DefaultFilename: "merged.pdf",
89			Filters: []runtime.FileFilter{
90				{DisplayName: "PDF Files (*.pdf)", Pattern: "*.pdf"},
91			},
92		})
93		if err != nil || path == "" {
94			return path, err
95		}
96		if !strings.HasSuffix(strings.ToLower(path), ".pdf") {
97			path += ".pdf"
98		}
99		return path, nil
100	}
101	
102	// MergePDFs interleaves pages from pathA and pathB and writes the result to outPath.
103	func (a *App) MergePDFs(pathA, pathB, outPath string, firstPageInA, reverseB bool, skipA, skipB []int, rotationsA, rotationsB map[int]int) error {
104		return mergePDFs(pathA, pathB, outPath, firstPageInA, reverseB, skipA, skipB, rotationsA, rotationsB)
105	}
106

  "new_string": "import { useState } from 'react'\nimport { Box, Group, Tabs, Text } from '@mantine/core'\nimport MergeMode from './components/MergeMode'\nimport SplitMode from './components/SplitMode'\n\ntype AppMode = 'split' | 'merge'\n\nexport default function App() {\n  const [mode, setMode] = useState<AppMode>('split')\n  const [splitInitialPath, setSplitInitialPath] = useState<string | null>(null)\n\n  const handleOpenInSplitMode = (path: string) => {\n    setSplitInitialPath(path)\n    setMode('split')\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <Box style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', height: '100vh', overflow: 'hidden' }}>\n      <Box\n        component=\"header\"\n        style={{\n          height: 48,\n          flexShrink: 0,\n          borderBottom: '1px solid var(--mantine-color-gray-3)',\n          display: 'flex',\n          alignItems: 'center',\n          paddingInline: 'var(--mantine-spacing-md)',\n        }}\n      >\n        <Group justify=\"space-between\" style={{ width: '100%' }}>\n          <Text fw={600} size=\"sm\">Paper Scan Processor</Text>\n          <Tabs value={mode} onChange={(v) => v && setMode(v as AppMode)}>\n            <Tabs.List>\n              <Tabs.Tab value=\"split\">Split</Tabs.Tab>\n              <Tabs.Tab value=\"merge\">Merge</Tabs.Tab>\n            </Tabs.List>\n          </Tabs>\n        </Group>\n      </Box>\n      <Box style={{ flex: 1, overflow: 'hidden' }}>\n        {mode === 'split'\n          ? <SplitMode initialPath={splitInitialPath} />\n          : <MergeMode onOpenInSplitMode={handleOpenInSplitMode} />}\n      </Box>\n    </Box>\n  )\n}"
}

1	import { useState, useEffect } from 'react'
2	import { Box, Button, Checkbox, Group, Modal, SegmentedControl, Text, Tooltip } from '@mantine/core'
3	import { IconAlertTriangle } from '@tabler/icons-react'
4	import { notifications } from '@mantine/notifications'
5	import { MergePDFs, OpenFile, SavePDF } from '../../../wailsjs/go/main/App'
6	import MergeModeThumbnailPanel, { DEFAULT_TOTAL_WIDTH, FirstPageIn, SelectedPage } from './ThumbnailPanel'
7	import DetailPanel from '../DetailPanel'
8	import { usePDFFile } from '../../hooks/usePDFFile'
9	import * as pageCache from '../../hooks/pageCache'
10	
11	function basename(p: string) {
12	  return p.split(/[\\/]/).pop() ?? p
13	}
14	
15	interface Props {
16	  onOpenInSplitMode: (path: string) => void
17	}
18	
19	export default function MergeMode({ onOpenInSplitMode }: Props) {
20	  const fileA = usePDFFile()
21	  const fileB = usePDFFile()
22	  const [selectedPage, setSelectedPage] = useState<SelectedPage>({ file: 'a', page: 1 })
23	  const [firstPageIn, setFirstPageIn] = useState<FirstPageIn>('a')
24	  const [reverseB, setReverseB] = useState(true)
25	  const [merging, setMerging] = useState(false)
26	  const [mergedPath, setMergedPath] = useState<string | null>(null)
27	  const [totalWidth, setTotalWidth] = useState(DEFAULT_TOTAL_WIDTH)
28	
29	  useEffect(() => {
30	    return () => {
31	      if (fileA.path) pageCache.evict(fileA.path)
32	      if (fileB.path) pageCache.evict(fileB.path)
33	    }
34	  }, [fileA.path, fileB.path])
35	
36	  // Subtract 22px to account for scrollbar + gap
37	  const colWidth = Math.floor((totalWidth - 22) / 2)
38	
39	  const handleChoose = async (file: FirstPageIn) => {
40	    const loaded = await (file === 'a' ? fileA : fileB).load()
41	    if (loaded) setSelectedPage({ file, page: 1 })
42	  }
43	
44	  const handleMerge = async () => {
45	    if (!fileA.path || !fileB.path) return
46	    const outPath = await SavePDF()
47	    if (!outPath) return
48	    setMerging(true)
49	    try {
50	      await MergePDFs(
51	        fileA.path, fileB.path, outPath,
52	        firstPageIn === 'a', reverseB,
53	        [...fileA.skipped], [...fileB.skipped],
54	        Object.fromEntries(fileA.rotations), Object.fromEntries(fileB.rotations),
55	      )
56	      setMergedPath(outPath)
57	    } catch (e) {
58	      notifications.show({ title: 'Merge failed', message: String(e), color: 'red' })
59	    } finally {
60	      setMerging(false)
61	    }
62	  }
63	
64	  const bothLoaded = fileA.path !== null && fileB.path !== null
65	  const unequalCounts = bothLoaded && fileA.count !== fileB.count
66	  const selectedFile = selectedPage.file === 'a' ? fileA : fileB
67	  const selectedPath = selectedFile.path
68	  const selectedCount = selectedFile.count
69	
70	  return (
71	    <Box style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', height: '100%' }}>
72	      <Modal opened={mergedPath !== null} onClose={() => setMergedPath(null)} title="Merge complete" centered>
73	        <Text size="sm" c="dimmed" mb="md">{mergedPath}</Text>
74	        <Group>
75	          <Button variant="default" onClick={() => { OpenFile(mergedPath!); setMergedPath(null) }}>
76	            Open in Preview
77	          </Button>
78	          <Button onClick={() => { onOpenInSplitMode(mergedPath!); setMergedPath(null) }}>
79	            Open in Split Mode
80	          </Button>
81	        </Group>
82	      </Modal>
83	      <Box
84	        style={{
85	          flexShrink: 0,
86	          borderBottom: '1px solid var(--mantine-color-gray-3)',
87	          display: 'flex',
88	          alignItems: 'stretch',
89	        }}
90	      >
91	        <FilePickerColumn label="File A" path={fileA.path} width={colWidth} onChoose={() => handleChoose('a')} />
92	        {/* Add 26 px to account for scrollbar + gap */}
93	        <FilePickerColumn label="File B" path={fileB.path} width={colWidth + 26} onChoose={() => handleChoose('b')} />
94	        <Group gap={8} px={12} style={{ flex: 1, justifyContent: 'flex-end' }}>
95	          {unequalCounts && (
96	            <Tooltip
97	              label={`File A has ${fileA.count} page${fileA.count !== 1 ? 's' : ''}, File B has ${fileB.count} page${fileB.count !== 1 ? 's' : ''}. The extra ${Math.abs(fileA.count - fileB.count)} page${Math.abs(fileA.count - fileB.count) !== 1 ? 's' : ''} will be appended at the end.`}
98	              multiline
99	              w={280}
100	            >
101	              <IconAlertTriangle size={18} color="var(--mantine-color-yellow-6)" />
102	            </Tooltip>
103	          )}
104	          <Checkbox
105	            size="sm"
106	            label="Reverse File B"
107	            checked={reverseB}
108	            onChange={(e) => setReverseB(e.currentTarget.checked)}
109	          />
110	          <Text size="sm" c="dimmed">First page in</Text>
111	          <SegmentedControl
112	            size="xs"
113	            value={firstPageIn}
114	            onChange={(v) => setFirstPageIn(v as FirstPageIn)}
115	            data={[
116	              { label: 'File A', value: 'a' },
117	              { label: 'File B', value: 'b' },
118	            ]}
119	          />
120	          <Button size="sm" disabled={!bothLoaded} loading={merging} onClick={handleMerge}>
121	            Merge & Save
122	          </Button>
123	        </Group>
124	      </Box>
125	
126	      <Box style={{ flex: 1, overflow: 'hidden', display: 'flex' }}>
127	        <MergeModeThumbnailPanel
128	          fileA={fileA}
129	          fileB={fileB}
130	          selectedPage={selectedPage}
131	          onSelectPage={(file, page) => setSelectedPage({ file, page })}
132	          firstPageIn={firstPageIn}
133	          totalWidth={totalWidth}
134	          onWidthChange={setTotalWidth}
135	          colWidth={colWidth}
136	          reverseB={reverseB}
137	        />
138	        {selectedPath && (
139	          <DetailPanel
140	            pdfPath={selectedPath}
141	            pageNum={selectedPage.page}
142	            pageCount={selectedCount}
143	            rotation={selectedFile.rotations.get(selectedPage.page) ?? 0}
144	            onNavigate={(page) => setSelectedPage({ file: selectedPage.file, page })}
145	            onToggleSkip={() => selectedFile.toggleSkip(selectedPage.page)}
146	            onRotate={() => selectedFile.rotate(selectedPage.page)}
147	          />
148	        )}
149	      </Box>
150	    </Box>
151	  )
152	}
153	
154	function FilePickerColumn({
155	  label, path, width, onChoose,
156	}: {
157	  label: string
158	  path: string | null
159	  width: number
160	  onChoose: () => void
161	}) {
162	  return (
163	    <Box
164	      style={{
165	        width,
166	        flexShrink: 0,
167	        padding: '6px 8px',
168	        borderRight: '1px solid var(--mantine-color-gray-3)',
169	        display: 'flex',
170	        flexDirection: 'column',
171	        justifyContent: 'center',
172	        gap: 2,
173	      }}
174	    >
175	      <Group justify="space-between" gap={4} wrap="nowrap">
176	        <Text size="xs" c="dimmed">{label}</Text>
177	        <Button size="xs" variant="default" onClick={onChoose}>Choose…</Button>
178	      </Group>
179	      <Text
180	        size="xs"
181	        style={{ overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }}
182	        title={path ?? undefined}
183	      >
184	        {path ? basename(path) : '—'}
185	      </Text>
186	    </Box>
187	  )
188	}
189

- **`SplitMode`**: `useEffect([initialPath])` auto-loads the path when provided

3	import ThumbnailPanel from './ThumbnailPanel'
4	import DetailPanel from '../DetailPanel'
5	import { OpenPDF, PageCount } from '../../../wailsjs/go/main/App'
6	
7	interface Props {
8	  initialPath?: string | null
9	}
10	
11	export default function SplitMode({ initialPath }: Props) {
12	  const [pdfPath, setPdfPath] = useState<string | null>(null)
13	  const [pageCount, setPageCount] = useState(0)
14	  const [selectedPage, setSelectedPage] = useState(1)
15	
16	  useEffect(() => {
17	    if (!initialPath) return
18	    PageCount(initialPath).then(count => {
19	      setPdfPath(initialPath)
20	      setPageCount(count)
21	      setSelectedPage(1)
22	    })
23	  }, [initialPath])
24	
25	  const handleOpen = async () => {
26	    const path = await OpenPDF()
27	    if (!path) return
28	    const count = await PageCount(path)
29	    setPdfPath(path)
30	    setPageCount(count)
31	    setSelectedPage(1)
32	  }
33	
34	  return (
35	    <Box style={{ display: 'flex', height: '100%' }}>
36	      {pdfPath ? (
37	        <>
38	          <ThumbnailPanel
39	            pdfPath={pdfPath}
40	            pageCount={pageCount}
41	            selectedPage={selectedPage}
42	            onSelectPage={setSelectedPage}
43	          />
44	          <DetailPanel
45	            pdfPath={pdfPath}
46	            pageNum={selectedPage}
47	            pageCount={pageCount}
48	            onNavigate={setSelectedPage}
49	          />
50	        </>
51	      ) : (
52	        <Box style={{ flex: 1, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center' }}>
53	          <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open PDF</Button>
54	        </Box>
55	      )}
56	    </Box>
57	  )
58	}
59

> AGENT

> DEVELOPER

Is it best practice to name functions exported by Wails PacscalCase? It looks weird in the TS code.

> AGENT

It's not a TypeScript best practice — PascalCase functions look like constructors or React components. It's just a consequence of Go requiring exported symbols to be PascalCase, and Wails generating bindings verbatim.

The common workaround is a thin wrapper module that re-exports everything with camelCase names:

```ts
// src/api.ts
export { MergePDFs as mergePDFs, OpenFile as openFile, OpenPDF as openPDF, PageCount as pageCount, RenderPage as renderPage, SavePDF as savePDF } from '../wailsjs/go/main/App'
```

Then all imports go through `src/api.ts` instead of directly to the generated bindings. The downside is one extra indirection layer and manual upkeep when new functions are added. Whether that trade-off is worth it is a matter of taste — many Wails projects just accept PascalCase and move on.

```
1	# Paper Scan Processor
2	
3	A desktop application for post-processing PDF files containing batches of scanned documents. The app has two modes: **Merge** and **Split**.
4	
5	## Tech stack
6	
7	- **Desktop framework**: Wails
8	- **Backend**: Go
9	- **Frontend**: TypeScript + React
10	- **UI components**: Mantine + `@mantine/dropzone`
11	- **Virtualization**: TanStack Virtual
12	- **Drag-and-drop**: dnd-kit
13	- **State**: Zustand
14	- **PDF manipulation**: pdfcpu
15	- **PDF rendering** (thumbnails): `mutool` (system install, called as subprocess)
16	
17	## Common elements
18	
19	Both modes include a thumbnail panel and a page detail panel, and support the same per-page editing operations.
20	
21	### Thumbnail panel
22	
23	A vertically scrolling strip of page thumbnails. Width is user-adjustable via a drag handle; thumbnails scale to fill the panel width. Rendering is on-demand and virtualized — only visible thumbnails (plus a small overscan buffer) are rendered at any given time.
24	
25	The Go backend exposes a per-page render method returning a base64-encoded PNG; the frontend requests thumbnails as they scroll into view (`mutool draw` subprocess).
26	
27	#### Keyboard shortcuts
28	
29	| Key                    | Action                                     |
30	| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
31	| `R`                    | Rotate selected page clockwise 90°         |
32	| `Shift+R`              | Rotate selected page counter-clockwise 90° |
33	| `Delete` / `Backspace` | Toggle skip on the selected page           |
34	| `←` / `→`              | Select previous / next page                |
35	
36	### Page detail panel
37	
38	Shows the currently selected page at reading resolution. Selecting a thumbnail updates it.
39	
40	Supports:
41	
42	- **Pan**: drag to pan.
43	- **Zoom**: scroll wheel or trackpad pinch to zoom in/out.
44	- **Navigate**: `←` / `→` to move to the previous/next page.
45	
46	Implemented with `react-zoom-pan-pinch`.
47	
48	### Page editing
49	
50	Individual pages can be edited in both modes before export or merge:
51	
52	- **Rotation**: pages can be rotated in 90° increments (clockwise or counter-clockwise).
53	- **Skip**: pages can be marked as skipped — excluded from the output but remaining visible in the thumbnail view (greyed out). A page is skipped by clicking a skip icon that appears on hover, or via keyboard shortcut.
54	- **Reorder**: pages can be reordered by dragging thumbnails to a new position.
55	
56	## Mode: Merge
57	
58	For scanners that can only scan one side at a time. The user scans all front pages as one PDF and all back pages as another, then uses Merge mode to interleave them into a single PDF.
59	
60	### Workflow
61	
62	1. The user loads two PDF files, labelled **File A** and **File B**.
63	2. The user selects which file contains the first page (**First page in: File A / File B**).
64	3. A **Reverse File B** checkbox controls whether File B is reversed before interleaving. This should be checked when the paper stack was flipped between scans (the typical case, when scanning one side at a time), causing the second-scanned pages to be in reverse order.
65	4. The app interleaves the pages: A1, B1, A2, B2, etc.
66	5. The user saves the merged result as a new PDF file on disk.
67	
68	The merged PDF can then be opened in Split mode for further processing.
69	
70	### Unequal page counts
71	
72	If File A and File B have different page counts, the app shows a warning before proceeding: "File A has X pages, File B has Y pages. The extra Z page(s) will be appended at the end." The user can cancel or continue. The extra pages from the longer file are appended in order after the interleaved section.
73	
74	### Layout
75	
76	Merge mode uses a two-column layout:
77	
78	- **Left panel** — two side-by-side thumbnail strips, one per input file.
79	- **Right panel** (fills remaining space) — the page detail view, showing whichever page was most recently selected in either thumbnail strip.
80	
81	The thumbnail strip for the file containing the second output page is offset downward by half a thumbnail height. This makes the interleaving order visually apparent: the two files' pages appear to slot between each other.
82	
83	```
84	  A               B
85	  ┌──────────┐
86	  │   A1     │
87	  └──────────┘  ┌──────────┐
88	                │   B1     │
89	  ┌──────────┐  └──────────┘
90	  │   A2     │
91	  └──────────┘  ┌──────────┐
92	                │   B2     │
93	  ┌──────────┐  └──────────┘
94	  │   A3     │
95	  └──────────┘
96	```
97	
98	The offset makes the interleaving order visually apparent without needing labels.
99	
100	### Error handling
101	
102	TBD.
103	
104	## Mode: Split
105	
106	### Workflow
107	
108	1. The user loads an input PDF via a file picker dialog or by dragging and dropping a file onto the app window.
109	2. The app displays all pages as thumbnails in the left panel. Clicking a thumbnail selects it and updates the detail panel on the right.
110	3. The user defines split points by clicking in the gaps between page thumbnails. A visual divider appears at each split point; clicking again removes it. Dividers can also be repositioned by dragging them to a different gap. Each divider marks where a new output file begins.
111	4. The user sets a global filename template using `{date}` (today's date as `YYYY-MM-DD`) and `{name}` (a per-file label). Example: `{date} {name}` → `2026-06-12 invoice.pdf`. The app prefills the filename for each output file using this template. The user can then edit any individual file's name before exporting.
112	5. The user sets a global output folder (an existing folder on the local filesystem, or a Google Drive folder if Drive integration is enabled). For each output file, the user can adjust:
113	   - The prefilled filename (editable)
114	   - The destination folder (overridable per file)
115	6. The user clicks Export. Before splitting, the app checks for filename conflicts at each destination. If any conflict is found, the export is aborted and an error message identifies the conflicting files. Once resolved, the app splits the input PDF and writes (or uploads) each output file. Afterwards, the app prompts the user to keep, move, or delete the input file.
116	
117	### Layout
118	
119	Split mode uses a two-column layout:
120	
121	- **Left panel** (adjustable width, drag handle on right edge) — a single vertically scrolling area that combines the thumbnail strip and output file controls.
122	- **Right panel** (fills remaining space) — the page detail view.
123	
124	#### Left panel structure
125	
126	The left panel is a continuous scroll area. Pages are grouped by output file. Each group is preceded by a compact **output file header** containing the editable filename field and (when applicable) destination folder. Split-point dividers between groups are the visual boundary between one output file and the next.
127	
128	```
129	┌─ invoice.pdf ──────────────────────┐
130	│ folder: /Documents                 │
131	└────────────────────────────────────┘
132	  [page 1 thumbnail]
133	  [page 2 thumbnail]
134	  [page 3 thumbnail]
135	  ──────── [gap / split point] ───────
136	┌─ receipt.pdf ──────────────────────┐
137	│ folder: /Documents                 │
138	└────────────────────────────────────┘
139	  [page 4 thumbnail]
140	  [page 5 thumbnail]
141	```
142	
143	Clicking a gap toggles a split point there and creates a new output file section. The filename and folder fields for each section appear immediately above its pages.
144	
145	### Keyboard shortcuts
146	
147	| Key     | Action                                                  |
148	| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
149	| `Space` | Toggle a split point after the selected page            |
150	| `Tab`   | Move focus to the next filename input in the left panel |
151	
152	### Google Drive integration (optional)
153	
154	- Authentication uses Google OAuth via a browser window, triggered the first time Drive is enabled. Credentials are stored locally and reused in future sessions.
155	- When Drive is enabled, each output file header in the left panel shows a Drive folder field. Clicking it opens a folder browser modal that displays the user's Drive folder tree. The user navigates the tree and selects a destination folder. The selected path is shown in the header (e.g. `My Drive / Clients / Smith / 2026`).
156	- The folder tree is fetched lazily — the first time the user opens the folder browser in a session.
157	- The folder browser shows a **recently used folders** list at the top for quick access. This list persists across sessions.
158	- The global output folder setting can be set to a Drive folder, which prefills all output file headers. Individual headers can be overridden.
159	- After export, each output file is uploaded to its designated Drive folder. If an upload fails, the affected header shows an inline error message and a Retry button; other uploads are unaffected.
160	- When Drive is enabled, the local subfolder name for each output file is derived automatically from the Drive folder path (see Local folder sorting below).
161	
162	### Local folder sorting
163	
164	Each output file can be assigned a subfolder name. The file is then saved to `[output folder] / [subfolder] / filename.pdf` rather than directly into the output folder.
165	
166	When Drive is enabled, the subfolder name is derived automatically from the innermost component of the Drive folder path (e.g. `My Drive / Clients / Smith / 2026` → subfolder `2026`). When Drive is disabled, the user enters the subfolder name manually in the left panel header.
167	
168	If two or more output files in the same batch share the same subfolder name, the app prefixes the parent folder name to disambiguate. If the parent name is also shared, it walks further up the hierarchy until uniqueness is achieved. Examples:
169	
170	- `Clients / Smith / 2026` and `Clients / Jones / 2026` → `Smith - 2026` and `Jones - 2026`
171	- `Clients / Smith / 2026` and `Archive / Smith / 2026` → `Clients - Smith - 2026` and `Archive - Smith - 2026`
172	
173	(When Drive is disabled and names are entered manually, the user is responsible for avoiding conflicts — no automatic disambiguation applies.)
174	
175	To ensure disambiguation is fully deterministic, **the output folder must be empty when the input PDF is opened**. The app enforces this and shows an error if the folder is not empty.
176	
177	### Persisted settings
178	
179	The following settings are saved across sessions:
180	
181	- Last-used output folder
182	- Filename template
183	
184	### Error handling
185	
186	TBD.
187	
188	## Implementation checklist
189	
190	### Primitives
191	
192	- [x] **Go: PDF merge/split** — interleave and split PDFs with pdfcpu; hardcoded paths; `_test.go` harness, no UI
193	- [x] **Frontend: Thumbnail panel** — virtualized vertical scroll, on-demand per-page render via Go/mutool, resizable width with drag handle
194	- [x] **Frontend: Detail panel** — pan and zoom with `react-zoom-pan-pinch`, driven by selected page
195	
196	### Merge mode
197	
198	- [x] **Step 1: End-to-end merge pipeline** — Go `OpenFileDialog` / `SaveFileDialog` RPCs; `MergePDFs` Wails RPC; minimal UI with two file-picker buttons and a Merge & Save button; no thumbnails
199	- [x] **Step 2: Thumbnail strips** — two-column layout using existing `ThumbnailPanel` primitive, one per file, side by side; Merge & Save moves to toolbar
200	- [x] **Step 3: A/B selector + visual offset** — "First page in" toggle (File A / File B); second strip offset down by half a thumbnail height
201	- [x] **Step 4: Reverse checkbox + page-count warning** — "Reverse File B" checkbox wired into merge call; detect unequal counts and show info in toolbar
202	- [x] **Step 5: Detail panel** — existing `DetailPanel` primitive on the right; selection in either strip updates it; `←` / `→` keyboard navigation
203	- [x] **Step 6: Page editing** — rotate and skip within the merge view, applied before the merge call
204	
205	### Split mode
206	
207	- [ ] **Go: render + split** — per-page PNG render and PDF split/export backend methods
208	- [ ] **Frontend: Split layout** — resizable left panel + detail panel
209	- [ ] **Frontend: Split points** — click gaps to toggle, drag to reposition, visual dividers
210	- [ ] **Frontend: Output file headers** — filename template, prefill, per-file folder override
211	- [ ] **Frontend: Export flow** — conflict check, keep/move/delete prompt after export
212	- [ ] **Page editing in Split** — rotate, skip, reorder
213	- [ ] **Keyboard shortcuts**
214	
215	### Google Drive integration
216	
217	- [ ] **OAuth authentication** — browser-based sign-in, credentials stored locally and reused across sessions
218	- [ ] **Folder browser modal** — lazy-loaded Drive folder tree, opens when clicking a folder field
219	- [ ] **Recently used folders** — shown at the top of the folder browser, persisted across sessions
220	- [ ] **Upload on export** — per-file upload to designated Drive folder, inline error + Retry on failure
221	- [ ] **Local subfolder derivation** — subfolder name derived automatically from the innermost Drive path component; disambiguation when multiple files share the same leaf name
222	
223	### Polish
224	
225	- [ ] **Persisted settings** — last-used output folder, filename template
226	
227	## Future / out of scope for v1
228	
229	- **Insert pages from another PDF**: allow the user to pull pages from a second PDF into the current document before splitting. Planned for v2.
230	- **Scanning integration**: trigger a scan from within the app using OS or device APIs. Not currently planned but under consideration.
231	- **Page reordering**: drag thumbnails to reorder pages before export or merge. No clear use case identified for the current workflow; omitted from v1.
232	
```
